[System 触发审查]:
Round 2 审稿报告独立审查请求 — Overall 仍 3.0，分数未动

Round 1 全部修改后分数不变。需要你独立评估我的策略判断和 Action Matrix。

---

## Holistic Pass

3 个 SYSTEMIC_ISSUE：
1. **[SYSTEMIC] 证据范围不足** (N2+W3+W6): 仅 LAMBADA 1 个 benchmark 的 per-example 分析。Round 1 的标题缩窄 + reframe 没有解决根因——需要扩展证据
2. **[SYSTEMIC] 方法论精度** (W1+W5+W7): 多处措辞过度（nearly disjoint, originate primarily, GDN 讨论不足）
3. **[SYSTEMIC] 新颖性/框架** (N4+W4): 标题写了 Predictive Multiplicity 但没用 formal metric

**核心判断**：分数不动的原因是**证据基太窄**，不是写作。唯一能实质提分的是扩展 per-example 分析 + 补充 formal metrics。

---

## Action Matrix 初步方案

### 高优先级（新证据）

**A1: ARC-Easy/MMLU Per-Example 分析** [补实验, Agent]
- 从 model zoo evaluation 输出提取 ARC-Easy 和 MMLU 的 per-example correctness
- 对 15 个模型（3 arch × 5 ratio）计算 helped/hurt/unchanged
- 计算 Jaccard + normalized ES across attention ratios
- **价值**：即使 convergence 不显著，如果 Jaccard 也 saturate，说明 decoupling 是 general phenomenon；如果不 saturate，也是有价值的对照
- **风险**：需要确认 lm-eval 输出中有 per-example 数据（非仅 aggregate score）
- 预估：纯数据处理，不需 GPU，如果数据已有则 <1 天
- **这是提分最关键的单项修改**

**A2: Formal Multiplicity Metrics** [计算, Agent]
- 从 Marx et al. (2020) 实现 ambiguity 和 discrepancy：
  - Ambiguity(x) = 1 if 至少 2 个 architecture 在 example x 上不同意
  - Overall ambiguity = fraction of examples with disagreement
  - 对每个 attention ratio 计算，看 ambiguity 如何随 attention 变化
- 可以在 LAMBADA + ARC-Easy/MMLU 上都做
- 预估：纯计算 <0.5 天
- **直接回应 W4 "标题写了 PM 但没用 formal metric"**

**A3: BH 校正分析** [计算, 已完成]
- BH adjusted p = 0.102，与 Bonferroni 相同。因为只有 1/6 benchmark 有小 p，BH 无法改善
- **论文修改**：加一句 "We also considered FDR control (Benjamini-Hochberg); the adjusted p-value (0.102) does not differ from the Bonferroni correction, as no other benchmark approaches significance."
- **rebuttal 回应 Q1**

### 中优先级（论文修改）

**A4: 补引 Fehlauer et al. (EMNLP 2025) + Sellam et al. (ICLR 2022)** [改 paper, Paper Agent]
- Fehlauer et al.: 同 venue 前一年，研究 seed variation 下的 convergence/divergence。差异化：他们研究 same-architecture different-seed，我们研究 cross-architecture same-training
- Sellam et al. MultiBERTs: 25 BERT seeds + per-example robustness。差异化：单架构多 seed vs 多架构单 seed
- **需要验证这两篇引用是否真实存在**

**A5: 标题进一步缩窄** [改 paper]
- 当前："Task-Specific Convergence and Per-Example Predictive Multiplicity in Hybrid Recurrent Architectures"
- 候选："...on LAMBADA" 或 "Evidence from Three Hybrid Recurrent Architectures on LAMBADA"

**A6: 措辞精度修复** [改 paper]
- W5: "nearly disjoint" → "low overlap, approximately twice the independence baseline"
- W7: "originate primarily in recurrent layers" → "are not explained by attention weight patterns, consistent with originating in recurrent layers"
- W2: Introduction 和 §4.2 scale 措辞一致性检查

**A7: GDN confound 进一步扩展** [改 paper]
- W1: 检查 §4.5 是否已充分。可能需要在 §4.1 Table 1 同时报告 with/without GDN 结果

### 低优先级

**A8: Contribution boundary** [rebuttal]
- W6: Dataset Cartography (EMNLP 2020) 和 MultiBERTs (ICLR 2022) 是纯分析论文被接收的先例
- Rebuttal point, 不需要修改论文

**A9: Novelty dispute** [rebuttal]
- N4: R1/R2 分歧。我们的差异化：architectural variation (not seed), dose-response (continuous variable), timescale separation (new finding), formal multiplicity metrics (if A2 完成)
- 做了 A2 后这个问题自动缓解

---

## 关键验证请求

1. **ARC-Easy/MMLU per-example 数据是否可用？** 需要确认 model zoo 的 lm-eval 输出中是否保存了 per-example correctness（不仅是 aggregate accuracy）。如果没有，需要重新跑 eval（~30 个 jobs）。
2. **Fehlauer et al. (EMNLP 2025) 真实性验证**——arXiv ID?
3. **Sellam et al. (ICLR 2022) "The MultiBERTs" 真实性验证**
4. **Round 1 修改为什么没提分？** 你的独立评估——是写作问题还是证据基问题？
5. **3.0 → 3.5 是否现实？** 如果 ARC-Easy/MMLU 分析 + formal metrics 都做了，你预期分数能动多少？

---

## BH 验证
已计算：6 个 confirmatory benchmark 的 BH adjusted p = 0.102（与 Bonferroni 相同）。BH 仅在有多个接近显著的 p 值时有效；我们只有 LAMBADA 一个 p=0.017，其余全 >0.233。Q1 建议无效。

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a major review request. The Director wants my independent assessment of why Round 2 didn't improve the score and whether the proposed strategy will work. Let me think through this carefully.

Key question: Why didn't the Round 1 fixes improve the score from 3.0?

My analysis:

1. **Round 1 fixes were primarily defensive** — they addressed weaknesses in the existing paper (floor effect, GDN confound, framing, CI language). These prevent score DECREASES but don't necessarily create score INCREASES. The paper's core evidence base didn't change.

2. **The fundamental constraint is evidence breadth** — the paper makes claims about "hybrid recurrent architectures" and "predictive multiplicity" but all per-example evidence comes from ONE benchmark (LAMBADA). This is not a writing problem; it's a data problem.
   
3. **Adding "Predictive Multiplicity" to the title without formal metrics backfired** — it raised expectations without delivering the analytical rigor to back them up, creating a credibility gap rather than strengthening the positioning.

Now evaluating the proposed actions: A1 (ARC-Easy/MMLU per-example analysis) is critical because it would show whether the decoupling pattern generalizes beyond LAMBADA, which would either validate the core finding or reveal it as benchmark-specific. The key constraint is whether per-example data from the model zoo evaluations is actually available.

A2 directly addresses the metrics gap by computing Marx's ambiguity and discrepancy measures from the per-example correctness data. A3 confirms BH correction doesn't help, which is useful for the rebuttal. A4 requires verification of the citations, particularly Fehlauer et al. which I'm less confident about. A5 (title narrowing) is less urgent since the current framing is already an improvement.

Now I need to verify the per-example data availability across all benchmarks—LAMBADA has it from exp-003-p2, but I should confirm ARC-Easy and MMLU also have log_samples from the original exp-001 runs. Sellam et al. (ICLR 2022) on MultiBERTs is solid, but I need to search for Fehlauer et al. (EMNLP 2025) to confirm it exists.

The core issue with Round 1 improvements is they were defensive rather than offensive—they patched weaknesses instead of expanding the evidence base, which is what's really limiting the paper's impact. If I can access per-example analysis across ARC-Easy and MMLU alongside LAMBADA, and if those results reveal interesting patterns or contrasts, the paper could realistically reach 3.3-3.5. The deciding factor is whether the multi-benchmark per-example story is compelling enough to justify the higher score.
```

```text
I found the paper you're looking for! Here's what I found:

The paper "Convergence and Divergence of Language Models under Different Random Seeds" by Fehlauer, Mahowald, and Pimentel was published at EMNLP 2025 in Suzhou, China. The paper investigates the convergence of language models trained under different random seeds, measuring convergence as the expected per-token Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence across seeds.

By comparing LM convergence as a function of model size and training checkpoint, the authors identify a four-phase convergence pattern: (i) an initial uniform phase, (ii) a sharp-convergence phase, (iii) a sharp-divergence phase, and (iv) a slow-reconvergence phase.

The researchers observe that larger models reconverge faster in later training stages, while smaller models never actually reconverge; these results suggest that a certain model size may be necessary to learn stable distributions.

The paper is available on the ACL Anthology at: https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1675/ and on arXiv at: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26643


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
I found the paper you're looking for! Here are the key details:

**Paper Information:**
"The MultiBERTs: BERT Reproductions for Robustness Analysis" was presented at the 10th International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR 2022).

**Authors:**
The paper was authored by Thibault Sellam, Steve Yadlowsky, Ian Tenney, Jason Wei, Naomi Saphra, Alexander D'Amour, Tal Linzen, Jasmijn Bastings, Iulia Raluca Turc, Jacob Eisenstein, Dipanjan Das, and Ellie Pavlick.

**Main Contribution:**
The paper introduces MultiBERTs, a set of 25 BERT-Base checkpoints, trained with similar hyper-parameters as the original BERT model but differing in random weight initialization and shuffling of training data. They also define the Multi-Bootstrap, a non-parametric bootstrap method for statistical inference designed for settings where there are multiple pre-trained models and limited test data.

**Resources:**
The authors released their models and statistical library along with an additional set of 140 intermediate checkpoints captured during pre-training to facilitate research on learning dynamics. The code and models are available on GitHub in the google-research language repository.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The results files contain only aggregate scores per benchmark, not per-example correctness data. There's no `log_samples` directory. The LAMBADA per-example analysis was done separately (perhaps by running with `--log_samples` flag specifically for LAMBADA).

This means:
1. **Per-example data for ARC-Easy/MMLU is NOT available** — only aggregate accuracy
2. To get per-example data, we'd need to rerun evaluations with `--log_samples` flag for all 15 models (3 arch × 5 ratio) on ARC-Easy and MMLU
3. Each eval job is ~10-30 min per model per benchmark with log_samples


4. That's 30 total jobs across both benchmarks, though they could be batched

I've already confirmed that per-example data doesn't exist for the non-LAMBADA benchmarks — I searched the entire project structure and found no `log_samples` directories outside of LAMBADA.

Getting per-example results for ARC-Easy and MMLU would require rerunning the full evaluations, which means GPU time and roughly a day of compute.

Now I'm thinking through the core strategic issue: why didn't the Round 1 revisions move the needle? The fixes were mostly defensive — they addressed specific weaknesses without introducing fundamentally new evidence or analysis. The core problem the reviewer identified, that the evidence base was too narrow, remained unchanged. Adding "Predictive Multiplicity" to the title without backing metrics probably raised expectations without delivering, creating a new gap between what the title promises and what the paper demonstrates. The title narrowing helped somewhat, but "Task-Specific Convergence" still carries implications about cross-task generalization that the work doesn't actually support.

The Jaccard analysis on ES and RetNet-HGRN2 is genuinely novel, but it's still confined to LAMBADA — it doesn't broaden the benchmark coverage. For a realistic path to 3.5, I'd need A1 and A2 together. If the per-example analysis on ARC-Easy and MMLU shows similar low Jaccard patterns and saturation effects, that would be strong evidence the multiplicity finding generalizes beyond LAMBADA, which could add 0.3-0.4 points. If those benchmarks show different patterns with higher overlap and no saturation, it's still informative as a contrast but less impactful, maybe 0.1-0.2 points.

A2 directly tackles W4 with formal metrics, adding another 0.1-0.2. Combined, I'm looking at 3.3-3.5 realistically, with 3.5 possible but not guaranteed.

The most compelling outcome would be if ARC-Easy and MMLU reveal the same low Jaccard despite lacking aggregate convergence — that would mean per-example divergence is universal while aggregate convergence is task-specific, which is a much stronger insight than just extending to more benchmarks. The real hypothesis here is that predictive multiplicity at the per-example level exists across all tasks, regardless of whether the aggregate metrics converge.

For A2, I'm looking at how Marx's framework applies here: ambiguity captures whether any architecture disagrees on a given example, and discrepancy measures the fraction of examples where at least two models diverge. With three architectures, each example has three binary predictions, so I can identify which examples show disagreement and quantify how widespread this divergence is.

The key insight is that as attention increases, discrepancy might stay relatively flat even if aggregate accuracy converges—meaning the total number of correct predictions stabilizes while the *specific* examples that different architectures get right shift around. The low Jaccard overlap supports this: architectures aren't reducing disagreement overall, they're just disagreeing on different examples. Computing Marx metrics across attention ratios would directly show this pattern of predictive multiplicity.

Now I need to write the review itself, starting with the main issues: I don't have per-example data available so I'd need to rerun experiments, both citations check out, and I need to explain why the first round didn't capture this angle.
```

```json
{
  "text": "[severity: warning] Round 2 Action Matrix 独立审查\n\n---\n\n## 验证回复\n\n### V1: ARC-Easy/MMLU per-example 数据 — 不可用，需重跑\n\n服务器上搜索确认：\n- `find log_samples -type d` = 空\n- `find *per_example*` = 仅 LAMBADA 的 2 个 csv\n- 所有 results.json 仅含 aggregate scores（acc, perplexity, stderr）\n\n**需要重跑 eval with `--log_samples` flag**。预估工作量：\n- 1.3B: 15 模型 × 2 benchmarks（ARC-Easy 2376 examples, MMLU ~14k examples）\n- 每个 eval job ~15-30 min（含 log_samples 输出）\n- 可并行，但只有 1 GPU → 串行 ~8-15 小时\n- 340M 如果也做：额外 15 模型 × 2 benchmarks\n\n建议：先只跑 1.3B ARC-Easy（2376 examples, 15 models, ~4-6 小时），如果 pattern 有价值再扩展 MMLU 和 340M。\n\n### V2: Fehlauer et al. (EMNLP 2025) — ✓ 真实\n\n\"Convergence and Divergence of Language Models under Different Random Seeds\"\nFehlauer, Mahowald, Pimentel. EMNLP 2025 Main. arXiv:2509.26643, ACL Anthology: 2025.emnlp-main.1675.\n研究 same-architecture different-seed 的 convergence，发现四阶段 pattern + model size 影响 reconvergence。\n**与我们的差异化清晰**：他们 same-arch different-seed，我们 cross-arch same-training。\n\n### V3: Sellam et al. (ICLR 2022) — ✓ 真实\n\n\"The MultiBERTs: BERT Reproductions for Robustness Analysis\"\nSellam, Yadlowsky, Tenney, Wei, Saphra, D'Amour, Linzen 等. ICLR 2022 Spotlight. arXiv:2106.16163.\n25 个 BERT checkpoints，Multi-Bootstrap 统计方法。\n**关键**：Alexander D'Amour 是共同作者——同时参与了 underspecification 和 MultiBERTs，完美的引用桥梁。\n\n---\n\n## V4: Round 1 为什么没提分？\n\n**我的独立判断：根因是证据基未扩展，不是写作问题。**\n\nRound 1 的所有修改都是**防御性的**——修补已有弱点（floor effect → normalized ES, GDN confound → RetNet-HGRN2, CI framing → complementary）。这些防止扣分但不创造加分。\n\n更严重的是，**标题改为 \"Predictive Multiplicity\" 反而造成了新的 expectation gap**：\n- 审稿人现在期待 Marx 式的 formal metrics（ambiguity, discrepancy）→ 论文没有 → 新缺陷 (W4)\n- 标题升级了 claim 范围但证据没变 → systemic issue #3 未解决\n\nRound 1 的分数分布：一部分修复了 Round 1 扣分项（+），另一部分引入了新期望（-），净效果 ≈ 0。\n\n---\n\n## V5: 3.0→3.5 可行性评估\n\n**条件估算**：\n\n| 场景 | 预期分数 | 条件 |\n|-------|---------|------|\n| 仅论文措辞修改（A3-A8） | 3.0-3.1 | 不够 |\n| + A2 formal metrics（LAMBADA only） | 3.1-3.2 | 回应 W4 但不扩展证据基 |\n| + A1 ARC-Easy per-example（pattern similar to LAMBADA） | **3.3-3.5** | 扩展证据基 + 最强提分杠杆 |\n| + A1 ARC-Easy per-example（pattern different） | 3.1-3.3 | 有价值的对照但不 compelling |\n\n**3.5 需要 A1 产出正面结果**。最有价值的发现是：ARC-Easy 上也有低 cross-arch Jaccard（per-example divergence is universal），即使没有 aggregate convergence。这会把论文从\"LAMBADA 上的观察\"升级为\"per-example divergence 是普遍现象，aggregate convergence 是例外\"。\n\n---\n\n## 策略评估\n\n### A1 是否值得做？— **值得，这是唯一能实质提分的项目**\n\n但我要指出一个更锐利的 hypothesis framing：\n\n**不要只是\"扩展到 ARC-Easy\"。核心 hypothesis 应该是：per-example divergence（低 Jaccard）在所有 benchmark 上都存在，不仅限于有 aggregate convergence 的 LAMBADA。** 如果成立，论文的 narrative 升级为：\n\n\"Predictive multiplicity is universal across task types. What is task-specific is not the per-example divergence (which is always present) but the aggregate convergence (which occurs only on retrieval tasks).\"\n\n这比\"LAMBADA 上有 decoupling\"强很多倍。即使 ARC-Easy 上 Jaccard 略高（比如 0.15 而非 0.10），只要远低于 J_max，论点就成立。\n\n### A2 值得做但不是优先\n\nA2 on LAMBADA alone 解决 W4 但对分数影响有限。建议：等 A1 的 ARC-Easy 数据出来后，A2 同时在 LAMBADA + ARC-Easy 上做 formal metrics。这样一次性解决 W4 + 扩展证据基。\n\n### A5 标题建议\n\n当前标题\"Task-Specific Convergence and Per-Example Predictive Multiplicity in Hybrid Recurrent Architectures\"——如果 A1 成功，可以保留不变（evidence 支撑了）。如果 A1 不做或失败，改为 \"...A Case Study on LAMBADA\" 是必要的。\n\n### A6 措辞修改是低成本高回报\n\nW5 \"nearly disjoint\" 和 W7 \"originate primarily\" 是每个审稿人都会注意到的过度措辞。改了不加分但不改持续扣分。\n\n---\n\n## 优先级建议（修改排序）\n\n1. **A1: ARC-Easy per-example**（唯一实质提分项，~6-8h GPU）\n2. **A2: Formal multiplicity metrics**（在 A1 数据出来后同时做 LAMBADA + ARC-Easy，~2h 计算）\n3. **A6: 措辞精度修复**（低成本，防止持续扣分）\n4. **A4: 补引 Fehlauer + Sellam**（均真实，增强 positioning）\n5. **A3: BH 一句话**（已完成分析，论文加一句）\n6. **A7: GDN 扩展**（如果 §4.5 已充分则 skip）\n7. **A5: 标题**（取决于 A1 结果）\n8. **A8+A9: rebuttal points**（最后准备）\n\n---\n\n## 遗漏问题\n\n**上一轮一致性检查的 §4.3 hurt normalized ES 值（0.047）仍需修正**。如果 Agent 已修正，需确认。",
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am operating as an independent scientific reviewer (7×24 continuous mode) for the `entrainment_gated_ft` project targeting EMNLP 2026. My role is to audit experiment results, verify statistical claims, check data consistency, review paper sections, and flag issues to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. All output must be in Chinese. I respond to `worker_done` and `roadmap_updated` notifications. I have read-only access to registry, SSH, W&B, and local files. I do NOT execute experiments or modify files.

   In this context window I completed: (1) reviews of confidence margin analysis (v1 failed, v2 done with methodological confound warning, within-category control verification), (2) decision review on whether to write confidence margin into paper (recommended yes), (3) Appendix I verification (found p-value bound error), (4) comprehensive Action Matrix review for Round 1 reviewer report (verified Jaccard formula, 5 citations, identified 2 data errors), (5) consistency check across all paper sections after Round 1 response modifications (found hurt normalized ES value error), (6) Round 2 Action Matrix independent assessment (identified per-example data unavailability, proposed sharper hypothesis framing), (7) ARC-Easy eval setup verification and data completeness confirmation.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Confidence margin within-category analysis**: Pure-model |loglikelihood| of target token predicts attention's per-example effect. Within pure-correct: hurt have lower confidence (d=0.83-0.92). Within pure-wrong: helped have higher target probability (d=0.73-0.77). Definitional confound between helped (pure wrong) vs hurt (pure right) was identified and resolved via within-category controls.
   - **Jaccard normalized ES**: J_max = min(|A|,|B|)/max(|A|,|B|). Normalized ES = (J_obs - J_null)/(J_max - J_null). Mean ~6%. Demonstrates overlap is at bottom of theoretically achievable range, not a floor effect.
   - **RetNet-HGRN2 only decoupling**: Without GDN, σ still converges (0.0162→≤0.0075, 54% reduction) and Jaccard still saturates (0.086-0.108). Non-monotonic σ trajectory (rebound at h6-1). Spearman ρ=-0.7.
   - **Predictive multiplicity (Marx 2020)** replaces underspecification as primary framework. Different architectures, similar aggregate performance, different per-example predictions. Formal metrics (ambiguity, discrepancy) still needed.
   - **Round 2 core issue**: Score stuck at 3.0 because Round 1 fixes were defensive (patching weaknesses) not offensive (adding evidence breadth). Evidence base still limited to LAMBADA only.
   - **ARC-Easy per-example eval**: All 15 models (3 arch × 5 ratio) evaluated with --log_samples on ARC-Easy (2,376 examples). Data available at `/root/entrainment_gated_ft/lm_eval_clean/arc_easy/{model_name}/samples/arc_easy.jsonl`. This is the key lever for 3.0→3.5.
   - **EMNLP 2026**: ARR unified review, 8-page main text limit, Overall Assessment scale 1-5. Target: Findings (3.0), aspiring to 3.5 (Borderline Conference).

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.../docs/paper/sections/experiments.tex`** (local)
     - Central file reviewed multiple times for consistency checking.
     - **§4.1** (L9): CI described as "complementary characterization" ✓
     - **§4.2** (L47-49): Scale described as "preliminary observation" ✓
     - **§4.3** (L55): New normalized ES text: "observed overlap reaches only 5--7\% of the achievable range above chance (normalized ES 0.065 helped, 0.047 hurt)" — **hurt value 0.047 is WRONG**, should be ~0.055 or ~0.062
     - **§4.3** (L57): Confidence margin sentence added: "However, pure-model confidence on the target token is strongly predictive within each architecture: hurt examples have lower target-token probability among pure-correct examples ($d = 0.83$--$0.92$), and helped examples have higher probability among pure-wrong examples ($d = 0.73$--$0.77$); see Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}."
     - **§4.3** (L57): Predictive multiplicity framing: "constitutes predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} arising from architectural variation rather than random initialization \citep{damour2022underspecification}"
     - **§4.4** (L66): Bootstrap CIs + within-arch Jaccard reference
     - **§4.4** (L68): Entropy per-architecture: GDN/HGRN2 |ΔH|≤0.002, RetNet ΔH=+0.018
     - **§4.5** (L72-76): Robustness with GDN exclusion + RetNet-HGRN2 decoupling: "all hybrid σ ≤ 0.008 (>54% below pure, ρ = -0.7)" — note >54% is technically 53.9%

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.../docs/paper/main.tex`** (local)
     - Title: "Task-Specific Convergence and Per-Example Predictive Multiplicity in Hybrid Recurrent Architectures"
     - Abstract: Uses "predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}", "complementary characterization"
     - Limitations: "complementary characterization", no "pre-registered" or "necessary condition"

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.../docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex`** (local)

     - Basu (2026) cited as \citep{basu2026routing} for content-based routing

     - D'Amour underspecification retained in its own paragraph as complementary

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.../docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex`** (local)

     - 2,376 examples per file (ARC-Easy test set)

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Confidence margin cross-category confound** (my finding): Original analysis compared helped (pure wrong) vs hurt (pure right), inflating Cohen's d by definitional confound. Fix: within-category controls (hurt vs stayed-right, helped vs stayed-wrong). Confirmed d=0.73-0.92 after correction. Director executed fix via worker.
   - **p-value bound error** (my finding): Paper claimed p < 10^{-55} but HGRN2 Control 1 p = 8.4e-55 > 1e-55. Fix: changed to p < 10^{-54} in both §4.3 and Appendix I L288. Verified via grep.
   - **§4.3 hurt normalized ES value** (my finding, STILL UNFIXED): Paper says "normalized ES 0.065 helped, 0.047 hurt" but 0.047 doesn't match any data value. Closest match: helped minimum (0.0474). Should be ~0.055 (overall mean) or ~0.062 (h3-1 mean). Reported to Director, listed as pending.
   - **RetNet-HGRN2 ">54%" claim** (my finding, minor): Actual reduction is 53.9%, so ">54%" is technically wrong by 0.1pp. Flagged as info.
   - **Basu citation year** (my finding): Director wrote "Basu (2025)" but arXiv ID 2603.20997 = March 2026. Should be Basu (2026). Flagged.
   - **SF-4 HGRN2 entropy error** (my finding): Director claimed HGRN2 ΔH=+0.0420 in Action Matrix but actual value from server data is 0.002 (20× difference). All three architectures have negligible ΔH. Flagged.
   - **Double "however" in §4.3** (my finding, minor style): Two "however" in same paragraph. Flagged as info.

   - Verified all 5 new citations (Marx 2020, Watson-Daniels 2023, Hsu & Calmon 2022, Waleffe 2024, Basu 2026)

   **Key strategic insight delivered**: The paper's score is stuck at 3.0 because evidence is limited to LAMBADA. The ONLY way to move the score is to expand per-example analysis to additional benchmarks (ARC-Easy). Adding PM title without formal metrics created a new expectation gap.

```json
{
```

```json
    "type": "file",
```

```json
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/sections/conclusion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\nWe present the first systematic per-example analysis of how attention affects cross-architecture agreement in hybrid recurrent models. Three findings emerge: convergence is task-specific (only LAMBADA among ten benchmarks); convergence depends on scale (complete at 1.3B, non-monotonic at 340M); and aggregate convergence and per-example divergence are decoupled---cross-architecture variance decreases gradually with attention, but per-example overlap saturates at the first attention level. This constitutes predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} from architectural variation: aggregate equivalence does not imply per-example interchangeability \\citep{damour2022underspecification}. The low-overlap per-example effect sets (Jaccard $\\leq 0.11$, approximately twice the independence baseline) suggest that ensembles of diverse hybrids may yield complementary coverage, and that deployment-critical applications should evaluate per-example agreement rather than relying on aggregate benchmarks alone. Future work could establish causal links through attention head ablation and test generality across other hybrid families.\n",
        "numLines": 4,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 4
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/sections/conclusion.tex"
  },
```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "0608b991-c737-493e-8d8c-3cef5a117107",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}\nThe design space for combining attention with recurrence has expanded along two axes: where mixing occurs and how much attention is allocated. Jamba \\citep{lieber2024jamba} interleaves Mamba and Transformer blocks across layers, while Griffin \\citep{de2024griffin} and RecurrentGemma \\citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} pair gated linear recurrences with local attention. RWKV-6 \\citep{peng2024eagle} adds matrix-valued recurrent states alongside attention-like token mixing, and Hymba \\citep{dong2024hymba} mixes attention and recurrence within individual heads rather than across layers. Mamba-2 \\citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a formal duality between structured state spaces and attention. Across these designs, comparative evaluation has relied on aggregate benchmark scores. Large-scale comparisons confirm that Mamba-2 hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs \\citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even a single attention layer creates latent structure enabling content-based routing \\citep{basu2026routing}. \\citet{wang2025hybrid} provide a controlled model zoo that holds training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio, which enables direct comparison. We use this zoo to study behavioral effects of attention at both aggregate and per-example granularity.\n\n\\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}\nLinear recurrent models evolved from structured state spaces to selective and gated variants. S4 \\citep{gu2022s4} introduced parameterized state-space layers with linear complexity for long-range dependencies, and \\citet{katharopoulos2020transformers} established a theoretical bridge by showing that softmax attention can be approximated by linear dot-product kernels. Mamba \\citep{gu2024mamba} extended S4 with input-dependent selection, which allows the recurrence to filter information based on content rather than position. Subsequent designs pursued different gating strategies: RetNet \\citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \\citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated linear recurrence with state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \\citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These three architectures serve as the recurrent backbones in our study, and their distinct parameterizations make them a natural test bed for whether attention produces uniform or architecture-specific behavioral effects.\n\n\\paragraph{Underspecification and Model Agreement.}\n\\citet{damour2022underspecification} formalized underspecification: a training pipeline admits multiple models with equivalent aggregate performance but different per-prediction behavior. Their study documented this across computer vision, medical imaging, and NLP, showing that standard model selection does not resolve the ambiguity. \\citet{mccoy2020berts} demonstrated a concrete NLP instance: BERT models trained from different random seeds diverge on specific linguistic constructions. \\citet{sellam2022multiberts} released 25 BERT checkpoints to quantify per-example robustness under seed variation. \\citet{fehlauer2025convergence} extended this line to autoregressive language models, finding a four-phase convergence pattern where larger models reconverge faster. \\citet{fort2019deep} and \\citet{kornblith2019similarity} further showed that different initializations converge to distinct modes with partially different representations. These studies attribute per-prediction divergence to variation in initialization or training noise within a single architecture; our setting replaces seed variation with architectural variation under matched training.\n\n\\paragraph{Predictive Multiplicity.}\n\\citet{marx2020predictive} introduced predictive multiplicity: the phenomenon that a prediction problem admits competing models with conflicting predictions on individual inputs. They proposed measures of multiplicity severity and showed that standard datasets produce substantially different predictions across near-optimal models. \\citet{watson2023predictive} extended this framework to probabilistic classification, and \\citet{hsu2022rashomon} introduced Rashomon capacity as a metric linking predictive multiplicity to the size of the near-optimal model set. These studies focus on models within a single hypothesis class that achieve similar loss. Our setting is analogous but the source of variation is architectural: different recurrent backbones with fixed training conditions produce conflicting per-example predictions despite similar aggregate scores. This places our work between underspecification \\citep{damour2022underspecification}, which attributes divergence to random initialization within a single architecture, and predictive multiplicity, which attributes it to the existence of multiple near-optimal solutions.\n\n\\paragraph{Ensemble Diversity.}\nThe relationship between prediction diversity and ensemble performance has a long theoretical history. \\citet{krogh1994ensembles} proved that ensemble error decomposes into average individual error minus a diversity term, so disagreement among members directly reduces ensemble error. \\citet{kuncheva2003measures} surveyed ten pairwise diversity measures and found that no single measure reliably predicts ensemble accuracy, which limits the practical use of diversity for ensemble selection. \\citet{lakshminarayanan2017simple} showed that deep ensembles trained from different random initializations produce well-calibrated uncertainty estimates, and that diversity arises naturally from stochastic optimization without explicit diversity objectives. The hybrid architectures we study are not ensembles, but the per-example disagreement we observe between architectures parallels the diversity that ensemble theory predicts should reduce combined error.\n\n\\paragraph{Per-Example Analysis.}\nA growing line of work treats evaluation examples as non-exchangeable, assigning per-example difficulty or informativeness scores. \\citet{swayamdipta2020dataset} introduced dataset cartography, which maps each training example into a confidence-variability space and identifies easy, ambiguous, and hard regions of a dataset. \\citet{rodriguez2021evaluation} applied Item Response Theory from psychometrics to NLP benchmarks, estimating per-item difficulty and discriminability that reveal how informative each example is about model quality. \\citet{ethayarajh2022understanding} proposed V-usable information as a measure of dataset difficulty relative to a specific model family, formalizing the intuition that difficulty depends on the learner. These methods characterize examples through the lens of a single model or model family. Our helped/hurt decomposition is a cross-architecture variant: we classify each example by how adding attention changes each architecture's prediction, then measure whether architectures agree on which examples are affected.\n\n\\paragraph{Attention Interpretability.}\nWhether attention weights carry interpretive signal has been debated. \\citet{jain2019attention} argued that attention distributions do not reliably indicate input importance, since alternative weight configurations can produce identical outputs. \\citet{wiegreffe2019attention} countered that alternative explanations do not invalidate attention-based ones under appropriate conditions. \\citet{clark2019what} showed that specific BERT attention heads capture syntactic relations such as coreference, even if the full attention matrix is not a faithful global explanation. \\citet{olsson2022induction} identified induction heads, attention heads that implement in-context copying by attending to prior occurrences of the current token's context. Our attention weight analysis is observational: we report differences in attention entropy and cosine similarity between helped and hurt examples but do not claim causal status for attention patterns.\n",
        "numLines": 23,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 23
      }
    },
```

```text
Let me review the ROADMAP for:
1. Research direction reasonableness

1. **ARC-Easy status outdated**: ROADMAP says "ARC-Easy evaluation" is "🔄 dispatched ~5h ago, checking status" and GPU is "当前跑 ARC-Easy evaluation" — but I've already confirmed the eval is COMPLETE (15/15 models done). The recovery worker also confirmed this and updated registry to DONE. The ROADMAP is stale on this point.


2. **D015 hypothesis is sound**: The per-example divergence being universal across task types while aggregate convergence is task-specific is testable now that I have the ARC-Easy data available.
3. **Critical open issues are correctly prioritized**: The power ceiling (R1-N1) is structural and can't be fully resolved, the n=3 bootstrap limitation (R1-N2) is acknowledged, and the single benchmark constraint (R2-N1) is being addressed with ARC-Easy.
4. **Timeline looks solid**: With 26 days until the ARR June deadline, there's plenty of time for the remaining ARC-Easy analysis and paper rewrite.
5. **GPU budget**: 

1× GPU is currently idle, which contradicts what the ROADMAP claims about it running the ARC-Easy evaluation.
6. **Missing from ROADMAP**: The §4.3 normalized ES value error (0.047 vs ~0.055) remains unfixed and untracked, and the ">54%" claim (actually 53.9%) also isn't documented.
7. **D015 is reasonable**: The fallback plan to add a scope qualifier if ARC-Easy doesn't show low Jaccard is honest, and the upgrade hypothesis is well-framed.

The main issues to flag are that the ROADMAP is stale—the ARC-Easy evaluation is already done, not in progress—and the GPU is sitting idle rather than actively running evaluations as documented. I should also check the decisions.yaml and review_issues.yaml to see what's actually being tracked versus what's missing.

The ROADMAP direction itself is sound and D015 is well-formed with a testable hypothesis and honest fallback plan, so I'm ready to post a focused review to the Director.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a critical result. Let me analyze it carefully before reviewing.

**Key findings from ARC-Easy per-example analysis:**

1. **Per-example classification**: 
   - Helped/hurt counts are much smaller than LAMBADA (144-206 vs ~400-500 for LAMBADA). This makes sense since ARC-Easy acc ranges ~0.70-0.74 vs LAMBADA ~0.48-0.52.
   - HGRN2 consistently benefits from attention (positive net in all ratios)
   - GDN and RetNet show mixed net effects
   
2. **Jaccard + Null Model**: All 24 comparisons are significantly above null (22/24 p<0.001, 2/24 p<0.01) with normalized effect sizes between 0.035–0.135, which is crucial for D015 since it shows the cross-architecture divergence in helped/hurt examples isn't specific to LAMBADA.

3. **Convergence σ**: Looking at how standard deviation changes across different head configurations—pure through h24-1, h12-1, h6-1, and h3-1—I'm not seeing the monotonic decrease I'd expect.

The correlation between σ and attention heads is essentially zero (ρ=-0.20, p=0.78), which confirms that unlike what we might see with aggregate metrics, there's no convergence pattern on ARC-Easy despite the per-example divergence being real.

4. **Multiplicity Metrics**: ARC-Easy shows α≈0.21 and δ≈2.7 compared to LAMBADA's α≈0.30 and δ≈0.55—so ARC-Easy has higher discrepancy but lower ambiguity, with both benchmarks showing substantial disagreement across roughly 20-30% of examples.

The D015 hypothesis is holding up: per-example divergence is definitely present on ARC-Easy with significant Jaccard overlap above null, even though the absolute overlap remains low.

Now I need to verify a few things—the σ values for ARC-Easy are substantially higher than LAMBADA's, which makes sense given it's a 4-choice task with ~0.70 accuracy and naturally higher variance. I should also confirm the artifacts saved correctly and double-check that the multiplicity metrics are computing as expected, where α represents the fraction of examples with at least one model disagreeing and δ captures the maximum confidence gap among models that do agree.
```

</details>

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me do a thorough independent verification of these results.

**1. Jaccard Sanity Check**

Let me pick one example and verify the math:
- h24-1, GDN-RetNet, helped: sizes [178, 158], intersection 21, J = 21/(178+158-21) = 21/315 = 0.0667 ✓
- h24-1, GDN-HGRN2, hurt: sizes [185, 119], intersection 30, J = 30/(185+119-30) = 30/274 = 0.1095 ✓


**2. Null Model Check**

Now I'm checking the null Jaccard for h24-1, GDN-RetNet_helped at 0.0365, but my initial calculation of the expected null doesn't match. I need to think through this more carefully—the null Jaccard under independence isn't just the product of marginals. The proper expectation involves the intersection and union sizes under random sampling, where for sets of size a and b drawn independently from N items, the expected intersection follows a specific formula.

Working through the math: the expected intersection is a*b/N and the expected union is a+b minus that intersection term. Plugging in the numbers for this case gives me exactly 0.0365, which confirms the observed value matches the null expectation perfectly.

**3. J_max Check**

The maximum possible Jaccard is simply the ratio of the smaller set size to the larger one, which for these two sets is 158/178 ≈ 0.8876.

**4. Normalized ES Check**

Now I'm calculating the normalized effect size...

**5. Multiplicity Metrics Check**

Looking at the disagreement distribution for ARC-Easy pure, the counts across all disagreement levels sum to exactly 2376 as expected, though I need to clarify what those distribution labels actually represent.

The labels indicate how many of the three architectures got each example correct: "3_of_3" means all three models were right (1434 examples, matching n_concordant_correct), "0_of_3" means all three were wrong (456), "1_of_3" means exactly one got it right (194), and "2_of_3" means exactly two got it right (292). From this, the ambiguity metric—the fraction where models disagree—comes out to (194 + 292) / 2376 = 0.2045, which checks out. Now I'm moving on to verify the discrepancy δ metric, which captures the spread in confidence scores among the concordant correct examples on ARC-Easy's multiple-choice format.

For ARC-Easy, the mean discrepancy of 2.55 represents the average loglikelihood spread across the three architectures when they all predict correctly, which seems plausible for this metric. But comparing across datasets, LAMBADA shows higher disagreement (α ≈ 0.307 versus ARC-Easy's 0.205) yet much lower discrepancy (δ ≈ 0.61 versus 2.55), which is striking. I'm realizing there's a potential issue here—Marx (2020) typically treats discrepancy as a probability-based measure, but the values here suggest they're using raw loglikelihood differences instead, and I need to reconcile whether that's the right interpretation for ARC-Easy's multiple-choice setup.

Looking at the actual numbers from ARC-Easy (loglikelihoods like -20.5, -29.0, -23.875, -23.375), a δ of ~2.5 nats makes sense as the maximum spread among architectures that agree. For LAMBADA's single-token generation, the lower δ (~0.6) is more concentrated, which aligns with how that task works. The key finding is that both datasets show comparable Jaccard indices and normalized expected support ranges, which supports the main hypothesis.

The critical distinction is that LAMBADA demonstrates monotonic aggregate convergence (σ decreases with model size, ρ=-1.0) while ARC-Easy shows no such trend (σ stays flat, ρ=-0.20 and not significant). This confirms the paper's central claim: per-example divergence appears across both tasks, but aggregate convergence is task-dependent. About 20% of ARC-Easy examples have at least one architecture disagreeing, indicating substantial multiplicity despite the above-null Jaccard overlap.

Now examining potential issues with the discrepancy metric...

Marx defines this in probability terms rather than loglikelihood, so I need to flag whether the analysis is using the exact formulation or a variant. The ARC-Easy helped/hurt counts are lower than LAMBADA's (150-200 vs 400-500) because the task is easier overall, leaving fewer examples where attention actually flips correctness. The "3_of_3" category dominates at 60% of examples, meaning the analysis operates on a constrained subset. There's also an asymmetry in HGRN2 behavior worth investigating.

Now I want to check if the analysis script is available on the server to verify how δ was actually computed.
```
